the vector obtained by stacking the functions inside the above expectations.

We treat (
C.32
)–(
C.35
) as a single system of equations,
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{\theta}(D)]=0
, in the single unknown
\theta
. This system is triangular, and can therefore be solved one block at a time: (
C.32
) involves only the coefficients
(\delta_{k},\beta_{k},\tau_{k},\gamma_{k})
; given these, (
C.33
) determines
(a_{k},b_{k})
; given those, (
C.34
) determines
\lambda
; and finally (
C.35
) determines
t
. Each block has a unique solution: (
C.32
) and (
C.33
) are normal equations of least-squares problems with nonsingular design (
Assumption

A.1
(iii)), (
C.34
) is the first-order condition of the strictly convex problem (
A.4
), and (
C.35
) is linear in
t
. The unique solution of the above system is given by

\theta^{*}:=\Big((\delta_{k}^{*},\beta_{k}^{*},\tau_{k}^{*},\gamma_{k}^{*})_{k=1}^{K},\ (a_{k},b_{k})_{k=1}^{K},\ \lambda^{*},\ \overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}\Big).

Indeed, the first block satisfies (
C.32
) due to (
A.2
); the second block solves (
C.33
) by
Assumption

A.3
and the tower property;
\lambda^{*}
solves (
C.34
) by the first-order condition in the proof of
Lemma

A.2
(which in turn follows from the proof of
Lemma

3.1
given in
Section

C.2
); and
t=\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
solves (
C.35
) by (
C.31
), since
w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})=\exp(g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*})/\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})
.

Algorithm

A.1
solves the empirical version of the same system,
\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi_{\theta}(D)]=0
, in exactly this order: Steps 1 and 2 of
Algorithm

A.1
solve the empirical versions of (
C.32
) and (
C.33
), Step 3 solves that of (
C.34
) (its first-order condition), and Step 5 solves that of (
C.35
), since

\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})=\exp(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda})/\mathbb{P}_{n}[\exp(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda})].

We denote this solution by
\widehat{\theta}
, and its last coordinate is the proposed estimator
\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR,lm}}
. It therefore suffices to show that
\widehat{\theta}
is consistent for
\theta^{*}
, and that the conditions of a standard asymptotic normality theorem for
Z
-estimators hold. We do so in the following steps.

Integrability requirements.

For any generic value
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})
of the block
((a_{k},b_{k})_{k=1}^{K},\lambda^{*})
in
\theta^{*}
, the map
X_{S_{\cap}}\mapsto g(X_{S_{\cap}};a^{\prime},b^{\prime})^{\top}\lambda^{\prime}
is an affine function with slope
\sum_{k=2}^{K}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}(b_{1}^{\prime}-b_{k}^{\prime})
. At
\theta^{*}
, this slope has norm smaller than
r/2
by assumption. Therefore, by continuity, there exists a neighborhood
\Theta
of
\theta^{*}
and constants
c,\varepsilon>0
such that

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\exp\big(2\,g(X_{S_{\cap}};a^{\prime},b^{\prime})^{\top}\lambda^{\prime}\big)\leq e^{c}\exp\big((r-\varepsilon)\|X_{S_{\cap}}\|\big).

(C.36)

Since
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(r\|X_{S_{\cap}}\|)]<\infty
, the right-hand side of (
C.36
), multiplied by any polynomial in
\|X_{S_{\cap}}\|
, is integrable. Together with
Assumption

A.1
(iv), this provides all the integrability we need below.

Consistency.

We show consistency in the same order as in the triangular system in
Eqs.

C.32
,
C.33
,
C.34
and
C.35
. First note that the least-squares estimators in Steps 1 and 2 of
Algorithm

A.1
are consistent by the law of large numbers and
Assumption

A.1
(iii)–(iv). Next, the empirical criterion
\lambda\mapsto\mathbb{P}_{n}[\exp(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda)]
is convex, and it converges in probability to
\psi_{P}(\lambda)
for every
\lambda
near
\lambda^{*}
, using the consistency of
(\widehat{a},\widehat{b})
, the mean value theorem and (
C.36
). Since
\lambda^{*}
is the unique minimizer of
\psi_{P}
(
Lemma

A.2
), it follows from
Newey and McFadden (1994, Theorem 2.7)
that, with probability tending to one, the empirical criterion has a local minimizer near
\lambda^{*}
that converges to
\lambda^{*}
in probability. By convexity, this local minimizer is a global minimizer, and it is unique with probability tending to one, because

\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\big]\succ 0,

where the last inequality (that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[g^{*}(g^{*})^{\top}]
is positive definite) follows from
Assumption

A.2
(i). Hence
\widehat{\lambda}\to\lambda^{*}
in probability. Finally, writing

\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR,lm}}=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\exp(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda})\,(\widehat{a}_{1}+X_{S_{\cap}}^{\top}\widehat{b}_{1})\big]}{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\exp(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda})\big]},

we obtain
\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR,lm}}\to\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
in probability from a uniform law of large numbers
(
Newey and McFadden, 1994
, Lemma 2.4)
, whose envelope condition holds by (
C.36
). We therefore have all the pieces to conclude that
\widehat{\theta}\to\theta^{*}
in probability.

Asymptotic normality.

We will apply
van der Vaart (1998, Theorem 5.41)
, which requires a few regularity conditions that we verify below.

1.

Smoothness.
The map
\theta\mapsto\psi_{\theta}(D)
is smooth. This holds here because the functions in (
C.32
) and (
C.33
) are affine in
\theta
, and the second-order derivatives of those in (
C.34
) and (
C.35
) are bounded on
\Theta
by a polynomial in
\|X_{S_{\cap}}\|
times
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\exp(g(X_{S_{\cap}};a^{\prime},b^{\prime})^{\top}\lambda^{\prime})
, which is integrable by (
C.36
).

2.

Second moments.

\mathbb{E}_{P}\|\psi_{\theta^{*}}(D)\|^{2}<\infty
, by
Assumption

A.1
(iv) and (
C.36
).

3.

Invertibility.
By the triangular structure of (
C.32
)–(
C.35
) noted above, the Jacobian
J:=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\partial_{\theta}\psi_{\theta}(D)]\big|_{\theta=\theta^{*}}
is therefore block lower-triangular, with diagonal blocks

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[(1,X_{S_{k}}^{\top},A,AX_{S_{k}}^{\top})^{\top}(1,X_{S_{k}}^{\top},A,AX_{S_{k}}^{\top})\big],\qquad-\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[(1,X_{S_{\cap}}^{\top})^{\top}(1,X_{S_{\cap}}^{\top})\big],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\exp(g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*})\,g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\big],\qquad\quad-\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[\exp(g^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*})\big].

The two on the first line are nonsingular by
Assumption

A.1
(iii), the third is positive definite by
Assumption

A.2
(i), and the last one is negative. Hence
J
is nonsingular.

Therefore,
van der Vaart (1998, Theorem 5.41)
applies to the consistent estimator
\widehat{\theta}
and yields that

\sqrt{n}\,(\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{*})=-J^{-1}\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi_{\theta^{*}}(D)]+o_{p}(1).

The last coordinate of
\widehat{\theta}
is
\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR,lm}}
, so that
\sqrt{n}\,(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR,lm}}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})=\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi_{\mathtt{SR,lm}}(D)]+o_{p}(1)
, where
\psi_{\mathtt{SR,lm}}(D)
denotes the last coordinate of
-J^{-1}\psi_{\theta^{*}}(D)
. Consequently, the desired result follows from the classical central limit theorem, with

\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR,lm}}={\rm Var}_{P}\big(\psi_{\mathtt{SR,lm}}(D)\big),

(C.37)

the last diagonal entry of
J^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi_{\theta^{*}}(D)\psi_{\theta^{*}}(D)^{\top}]\,(J^{-1})^{\top}
. This finishes the proof.

\square

C.13
Proof of
Lemma

B.1

Proof.

As in (
A.4
),
\lambda^{*}
minimizes

\psi_{P}(\lambda)=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\lambda(\alpha+\beta X_{1}))]=\exp(\lambda\alpha+\lambda^{2}\beta^{2}/2)
. This is strictly convex in

\lambda
, and setting the derivative to zero gives
\alpha+\lambda^{*}\beta^{2}=0
,
i.e.,
\lambda^{*}=-\alpha/\beta^{2}
.

Lemma

A.2

then yields

w^{*}(X_{1})=\frac{e^{\lambda^{*}(\alpha+\beta X_{1})}}{\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})}=\exp\left(\lambda^{*}\beta X_{1}-\frac{1}{2}(\lambda^{*}\beta)^{2}\right).

Recognizing this as the likelihood ratio of
\mathcal{N}(c,1)
w.r.t.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
c=\lambda^{*}\beta
, we conclude that

X_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(c,1)
under
w^{*}P
. Finally, invoking
Lemma

A.1
and
Assumption

A.3
,

\displaystyle\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}=\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})]=\mathbb{E}_{w^{*}P}[a_{1}+b_{1}X_{1}]=a_{1}+b_{1}c,

which is the same as
a_{2}+b_{2}c
, since
\alpha+\beta c=0
.

\square

C.14
Proof of
Lemma

B.2

Proof.

Recall that in
Example

1
, we have
Y=1+X_{1}-X_{2}+\varepsilon_{Y}
on
\{A=1\}
and
Y=4X_{2}+\varepsilon_{Y}
on
\{A=0\}
. We begin with the contrast that is easier to compute: Since the linear regression with adjustment set
S_{2}
is correctly specified,

\displaystyle\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=1,X_{1},X_{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=0,X_{1},X_{2}]

\displaystyle=(1+X_{1}-X_{2})-4X_{2}=1+X_{1}-5X_{2}.

Thus,
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})\mid X_{1}]=1+X_{1}
since

\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{2}\mid X_{1}]=0
. In the notation of
Assumption

A.3
, we showed that
a_{2}=b_{2}=1
.

To compute
\tau^{*}(X_{1})
, we need the
L^{2}
projection of
X_{2}
on
X_{1}
under
P(\cdot\mid A=a)
; denote it by
\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[X_{2}\mid X_{1},A=a]
. We will use the following formula:

\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[X_{2}\mid X_{1},A=a]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{2}\mid A=a]+\frac{{\rm Cov}(X_{1},X_{2}\mid A=a)}{{\rm Var}(X_{1}\mid A=a)}(X_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}\mid A=a]).

(C.38)

Let us now compute the moments involved in the above formula. Write
e(x)=1/(1+e^{5x_{1}+5x_{2}})

for the propensity score. Using
e(-x)=1-e(x)
and

(X_{1},X_{2})\overset{d}{=}(-X_{1},-X_{2})
, we deduce that for any bounded measurable function
h
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X)\mathbf{1}\{A=1\}]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X)e(X)]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(-X)e(-X)]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(-X)\mathbf{1}\{A=0\}].

Taking
h\equiv 1
gives
P(A=1)=P(A=0)=\tfrac{1}{2}
, and hence

\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X)\mid A=1]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(-X)\mid A=0]
for all bounded
h
. When
h
is an even function,
the two group means agree, and are equal to
\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X)]
. If
h
is an odd function, then the two group means are negatives of each other. Applying this to

h(x)=x_{1}^{2}
and
h(x)=x_{1}x_{2}
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}^{2}\mid A=a]=1,\qquad\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}X_{2}\mid A=a]=0\qquad(a=0,1).

Similarly, using
h(x)=x_{1}
and
h(x)=x_{2}
we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}\mid A=0]=-\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}\mid A=1]
and likewise for
X_{2}
. Finally, since
e
is
symmetric in its two arguments and
(X_{1},X_{2})
is exchangeable, we have

(X_{1},X_{2},A)\overset{d}{=}(X_{2},X_{1},A)
and thus
\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{2}\mid A=1]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}\mid A=1]=:p
.
To summarize,

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{1}\mid A=a]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{2}\mid A=a]=\pm p,\ {\rm Var}(X_{1}\mid A=a)=1-p^{2},\ {\rm Cov}(X_{1},X_{2}\mid A=a)=-p^{2},\end{gathered}

with
+
for
a=1
and
-
for
a=0
.

Returning to the main proof, we use (
C.38
) to conclude that the
L^{2}
projection of
X_{2}
on
X_{1}
under
P(\cdot\mid A=a)
is given by

\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[X_{2}\mid X_{1},A=a]=\begin{cases}\displaystyle p-\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}\big(X_{1}-p\big)=\frac{p}{1-p^{2}}-\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}X_{1}&\text{if }a=1,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle-p-\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}\big(X_{1}+p\big)=-\frac{p}{1-p^{2}}-\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}X_{1}&\text{if }a=0.\end{cases}

Consequently,

\displaystyle\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle=\Big[1+X_{1}-\frac{p}{1-p^{2}}+\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}X_{1}\Big]-4\Big[-\frac{p}{1-p^{2}}-\frac{p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}X_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\frac{3p}{1-p^{2}}\Big)+\Big(1+\frac{5p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}\Big)X_{1}.

Since
\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})
is already a function of
X_{1}
, and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})\mid X_{1}]=1+X_{1}
,

g^{*}(X_{1})=\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})\mid X_{1}]=\frac{3p}{1-p^{2}}+\frac{5p^{2}}{1-p^{2}}X_{1}.

Finally, applying
Lemma

B.1
with
\alpha=3p/(1-p^{2})
and
\beta=5p^{2}/(1-p^{2})
we deduce that
c=-\alpha/\beta=-3/(5p)
, and
w^{*}(X_{1})=\exp(cX_{1}-c^{2}/2)
, i.e.,
X_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(c,1)
under
w^{*}P
. Moreover,

\lambda^{*}=-\alpha/\beta^{2}=-3(1-p^{2})/(25p^{3})
and
using the last part of
Lemma

B.1
with
a_{2}=b_{2}=1
, we conclude that

\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}=1+c=1-3/(5p)
.

\square

C.15
Proof of
Lemma

B.3

Proof.

Recall that in
Example

2
we have
Y=1+\tfrac{1}{2}X_{1}-U_{2}+\varepsilon_{Y}
on
\{A=1\}
and

Y=5U_{2}+\varepsilon_{Y}
on
\{A=0\}
, where
A=\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{X_{1}+U_{1}>0\right\}}
,
X_{2}=U_{1}+U_{2}
, and
X_{1},U_{1},U_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. We begin with the contrast that is easier to compute: Since the linear regression with adjustment set
S_{1}
is correctly specified,

\displaystyle\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=1,X_{1}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=0,X_{1}]=1+\frac{1}{2}X_{1},

using that
U_{2}\perp\!\!\!\!\perp\{A=a,X_{1}\}
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[U_{2}]=0
.
Consequently,
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{1}]=1+\frac{1}{2}X_{1}
. In the notation of
Assumption

A.3
, we showed that
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,\frac{1}{2})
.

Next, let
X_{S_{2}}
denote
(X_{1},X_{2})
. To compute
\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})
, we need the
L^{2}
projection of
U_{2}
on
X_{S_{2}}
under
P(\cdot\mid A=a)
; denote it by
\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[U_{2}\mid X_{S_{2}},A=a]
. We will use the following formula, which is the vector analog of (
C.38
).

\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[U_{2}\mid X_{S_{2}},A=a]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[U_{2}\mid A=a]\\
+{{\rm Cov}(U_{2},X_{S_{2}}\mid A=a)}{{\rm Var}(X_{S_{2}}\mid A=a)}^{-1}(X_{S_{2}}-\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{S_{2}}\mid A=a]).

(C.39)

Let us now compute the moments used in the above formula. Since
X_{1}+U_{1}\sim N(0,2)
, and
A=\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{X_{1}+U_{1}>0\right\}}
, integration by parts yields

\mathbb{E}_{P}[(X_{1}+U_{1})\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{z}{\sqrt{2}}\,\phi\left(\frac{z}{\sqrt{2}}\right)dz=-\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi^{\prime}(z)\,dz=\sqrt{2}\,\phi(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}},

where
\phi(\cdot)
denotes the density function of standard Gaussian. On the other hand, using the symmetry of
X_{1}+U_{1}
about zero,

\mathbb{E}_{P}[(X_{1}+U_{1})^{2}\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}]=1,\quad\text{and}\quad P(A=1)=\frac{1}{2}.

Next, denote
V:=(X_{1},X_{2},U_{2})^{\top}
,
\vartheta:=\frac{1}{2}{\rm Cov}(V,X_{1}+U_{1})=\frac{1}{2}(1,1,0)^{\top}
. Observe that
V^{\perp}:=V-\vartheta(X_{1}+U_{1})
is uncorrelated with
X_{1}+U_{1}
, and therefore independent of
X_{1}+U_{1}
(and hence
V^{\perp}\perp\!\!\!\!\perp A
). Using this, together with the above displays and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[V^{\perp}]=0
, we deduce that

\mathbb{E}_{P}[V\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}]=\frac{\vartheta}{\sqrt{\pi}},\qquad\mathbb{E}_{P}[VV^{\top}\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}]=\vartheta\vartheta^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}{\rm Var}(V^{\perp})=\frac{1}{2}{\rm Var}(V),

where in the last step we used
{\rm Var}(V^{\perp})={\rm Var}(V)-2\vartheta\vartheta^{\top}
. Repeating the above with
1-\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}
in place of
\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{A=1\right\}}
, and finally dividing with
P(A=1)=\frac{1}{2}
, we conclude that

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}_{P}[V\mid A=a]=\pm\frac{2\vartheta}{\sqrt{\pi}}=\pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}(1,1,0)^{\top},\\
{\rm Var}(V\mid A=a)={\rm Var}(V)-\frac{4\vartheta\vartheta^{\top}}{\pi}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&2&1\\
0&1&1\end{pmatrix}-\frac{1}{\pi}\begin{pmatrix}1&1&0\\
1&1&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix},\end{gathered}

(C.40)

with
+
for
a=1
and
-
for
a=0
. After substituting these into (
C.39
) and simplifying, we obtain

\mathbb{E}_{P}^{*}[U_{2}\mid X_{1},X_{2},A=a]=\frac{X_{1}+(\pi-1)X_{2}+(-1)^{a}\sqrt{\pi}}{2\pi-3}.

Consequently,

\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})=\left[1+\frac{1}{2}X_{1}-\frac{X_{1}+(\pi-1)X_{2}-\sqrt{\pi}}{2\pi-3}\right]-5\,\frac{(X_{1}+(\pi-1)X_{2}+\sqrt{\pi})}{2\pi-3},

and thus, using
\mathbb{E}_{P}[X_{2}\mid X_{1}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})\mid X_{1}]=\Big(1-\frac{4\sqrt{\pi}}{2\pi-3}\Big)+\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{6}{2\pi-3}\Big)X_{1}.

Combining this with
\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})=\tau^{*}(X_{1})=1+\frac{1}{2}X_{1}
, we have

g^{*}(X_{1})=\tau^{*}(X_{S_{1}})-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau^{*}(X_{S_{2}})\mid X_{1}]=\frac{4\sqrt{\pi}+6X_{1}}{2\pi-3}.

Finally, applying

Lemma

B.1
with
\alpha=4\sqrt{\pi}/(2\pi-3)
and
\beta=6/(2\pi-3)
, we deduce that
X_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(c,1)
under
w^{*}P
where
c=-\alpha/\beta=-2\sqrt{\pi}/3
. Moreover,

\lambda^{*}=-\alpha/\beta^{2}=-\sqrt{\pi}(2\pi-3)/9
, and using the last part of
Lemma

B.1
with
(a_{1},b_{1})=(1,\frac{1}{2})
, we conclude that

\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}=1+\frac{1}{2}c=1-\sqrt{\pi}/3
. This finishes the proof.

\square

C.16
Proof of
Lemma

B.6

Proof.

Since
Assumption

B.3
implies
Assumption

3
(i) for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, the existence and uniqueness of
\lambda^{*}(P)
directly follow from
Lemma

3.1
(see
Section

C.2
for a proof). It remains to derive the bound on
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\lambda^{*}(P)\|
. Fix any
P\in\mathcal{P}
and assume
\lambda^{*}(P)\neq 0
(otherwise the claim is trivial). Write
\lambda^{*}(P)=tv
with
t=\|\lambda^{*}(P)\|
and
\|v\|=1
. Since
\psi_{P}(0)=1
, we have
\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*}(P))\leq 1
by definition, and therefore

1\geq\psi_{P}(tv)\geq\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\exp\left\{t\,v^{\top}g\right\}\text{ $\mathbf{1}$}{\left\{v^{\top}g\geq\zeta\right\}}\right]\geq\exp\left\{t\zeta\right\}P\big(v^{\top}g\geq\zeta\big)\geq c_{0}\,\exp\left(t\zeta\right).

Rearranging gives
t\leq\log(1/c_{0})/\zeta
. Since this holds for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, we are through.

\square

C.17
Proof of
Theorem

B.7

Proof.

The proof mirrors that of
Theorem

3.2
in
Section

C.3
,
with the key modification being that we now extend the supporting results
Lemmas

D.5
,
D.1
,
D.4
,
D.2
,
D.3
,
D.6
and
D.7
from
Appendix

D

uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.
We organize the proof in three steps:
(i) uniform extensions of the supporting rate results,
(ii) a uniform asymptotic linear expansion for

\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}
,
and (iii) a Berry–Esseen bound,
which together yield the desired conclusions. Note that each of the quantities
g
,
\lambda^{*}
,
w^{*}
,
\nu^{*}
,
\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
and
\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}
will now include
P
as an additional argument.

Uniform versions of the supporting results.

Our proofs of
Lemmas

D.5
,
D.1
,
D.4
,
D.2
,
D.3
,
D.6
and
D.7
primarily rely on three ingredients:
(a) Hölder’s inequality,
(b) finite moment generating function of
g(X_{S_{\cap}};\,P)
in a neighborhood of
\lambda^{*}(P)
, and (c) the
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
rates on the nuisance functions. We can thus extend all of these supporting results to hold uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}
under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, as follows.

1.

Lemma

D.8
shows that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}};\,P)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

2.

Lemma

D.9
shows that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}(P)\|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

3.

Lemma

D.12
shows that
for each
k=1,\dots,K
,

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\!\bigl[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\bigl(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(D)-\tau_{k}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)\bigr)\bigr]\right|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and the same holds with
(\widehat{w}-w^{*})
replacing
w^{*}
.

4.

Lemma

D.10
shows that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};\,P)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

5.

Lemma

D.11
shows that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}(P)\|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

6.

Lemma

D.13
shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\,\widehat{\lambda}-g(X_{S_{\cap}};P)^{\top}\lambda^{*}(P)\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]+R_{n}^{w}(P),

where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu,P):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}};P)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)
, and the remainder term is uniformly asymptotically negligible:
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|R_{n}^{w}(P)|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

7.

Lemma

D.14
shows that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left|B_{n}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}(P)\right|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where

\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}(P)=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)(\Delta\tau^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)-\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}}))\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right].

Uniform asymptotic linear expansion.

For each
P\in\mathcal{P}
, define the influence function

\begin{split}\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;P)&:=w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\,\Bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu^{*}_{k}\bigl(\tau_{k}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)\bigr)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(\lambda^{*})^{\top}\bigl(\Delta\tau^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)-g(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\bigr)\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\Bigg],\end{split}

(C.41)

where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu,P):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}};P)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)
.
It follows from
Assumption

B.2
that

\mathbb{E}_{P}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;\,P)]=0
.
The algebraic decompositions used in the proof of
Theorem

3.2
(cf.
Section

C.3
) carry over verbatim to give

\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)\;=\;\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;P)]\;+\;r_{n}(P),

(C.42)

where
r_{n}(P)
is the sum of all the remainder terms from
the proof in
Section

C.3
.
We show below that
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left|r_{n}(P)\right|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by
examining each of these remainder terms in turn.

1.

AIPW approximation errors.

These are the terms
\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(D)-\tau_{k}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P))]

and the cross-term with
(\widehat{w}-w^{*})
;
both are
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
uniformly by
Lemma

D.12
. This tells us that the
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
term in (
C.2
) is asymptotically negligible uniformly in
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

2.

Oracle estimators aggregated with
\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}
.
The remainder term in (
C.4
) is uniformly asymptotically negligible since
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}(P)\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
from
Lemma

D.11
and for each
P\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)(\Delta\tau^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)-g(X_{S_{\cap}};P))]=0
by definition.

3.

Sum of cross-terms
(\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*})(\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}_{k}-\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{R},*}_{k})
.
We apply
Lemma

D.10
to deduce that the remainder term in (
C.5
) is uniformly asymptotically negligible. This, combined with (
C.2
) and
Lemma

D.11
tells us that the sum of the cross-terms in (
C.6
) is uniformly asymptotically negligible.

Starting from the decomposition in (
C.3
) and combining the above three items, we arrive at

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-B_{n}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}^{*}(P)\,\widehat{\tau}_{k}^{\,\mathtt{R},*}(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\right)\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where the remainder term is uniformly asymptotically negligible over
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

4.

Taylor expansion of transfer weights.
Using
Lemma

D.13
, we can continue from the last display to write

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-B_{n}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}^{*}(P)\widehat{\tau}_{k}^{\,\mathtt{R},*}(P)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\,\widehat{\lambda}-g(X_{S_{\cap}};P)^{\top}\lambda^{*}(P)\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]+R_{n}^{w}(P),

where
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|R_{n}^{w}(P)|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

5.

Bias-correction approximation.
It follows from
Lemma

D.9
that the remainder term in (
C.10
) is uniformly asymptotically negligible. Then, using
Lemma

D.14
, we deduce from the last display that the remainder term in (
C.11
) is uniformly
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, which gives us the desired asymptotic linear expansion as in (
C.42
).

Berry–Esseen bound.

As in the statement of
Theorem

B.7
, we define

\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P):={\rm Var}_{P}(\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;\,P))
. We apply the Berry–Esseen theorem in its
(2+\delta)
-moment form (see, e.g.,
Petrov (1975, Chapter V, Theorem 5)
): For i.i.d. mean-zero variables
\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D_{i}\,;P)
with
finite
(2+\delta)
-th moment,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;P)]}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\;\leq\;\frac{C_{\delta}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;P)|^{2+\delta}]}{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)^{(2+\delta)/2}\,n^{\delta/2}}.

(C.43)

Bounding the supremum over
{P\in\mathcal{P}}
of the right-hand side requires establishing a uniform upper bound on

\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}|^{2+\delta}]
, since we have
\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)\geq v_{0}>0
by assumption. Write

\Lambda:=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\lambda^{*}(P)\|<\infty
(finite by
Lemma

B.6
). Consider the decomposition

\psi^{\mathrm{SR}}(D;P)=w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\big[\Phi_{1}(D;P)+\Phi_{2}(D;P)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\big],

where
\Phi_{1}:=\sum_{k}\nu_{k}^{*}(\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})
and
\Phi_{2}:=(\lambda^{*})^{\top}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g)
. First, we show that

\sup_{P}\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\Phi_{1}|^{q}+|\Phi_{2}|^{q}]<\infty.

(C.44)

Towards showing the above, note that the strong overlap condition implies that

|\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)|\leq(1+\xi^{-1})(|\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})|+|\mu_{0}(X_{S_{k}})|)+2\xi^{-1}|Y|.

Also, strong overlap together with the conditional Jensen inequality yields that

\mathbb{E}_{P}|\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})|^{q}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}[A|Y|^{q}/e(X_{S_{k}})]\leq\xi^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}|Y|^{q},

and similarly for
\mu_{0}
. Note also that
g
has uniformly bounded moments due to condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
.
\nu^{*}(P)
and
\lambda^{*}(P)
are uniformly bounded by
Lemma

D.11
and
Lemma

B.6
, respectively. Finally,
\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)
is uniformly bounded due to the inequality
|\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)|\leq\|w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(P)}\|m_{1}\|_{L^{2}(P)}
. This finishes the proof of (
C.44
).

Second, observe that
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)=m_{1}-g^{\top}\nu^{*}_{2:K}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
has uniformly bounded moments of every order due to condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
. Moreover, Jensen’s inequality yields

\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*}(P))\geq\exp(\lambda^{*}(P)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{P}[g])\geq\exp(-\Lambda\sup_{P}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|g\|),

and therefore, for every
s<r/\Lambda
, we have

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)^{s}\big]\leq\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{s\Lambda\|g\|}]}{\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*}(P))^{s}}<\infty.

Now choose
\delta\in(0,q-2)
small enough such that
s_{\delta}:=(2+\delta)q/(q-2-\delta)<r/\Lambda
. Such a choice is possible because
s_{\delta}\to 2q/(q-2)<r/\Lambda
as
\delta\downarrow 0
, by condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
. Then, applying Hölder’s inequality with exponents
q/(2+\delta)
and
q/(q-2-\delta)
gives

\mathbb{E}_{P}|\psi^{\mathrm{SR}}|^{2+\delta}\leq 2^{1+\delta}\Big\{(\mathbb{E}_{P}|\Phi_{1}|^{q})^{\frac{2+\delta}{q}}(\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*})^{s_{\delta}}])^{\frac{q-2-\delta}{q}}+(\mathbb{E}_{P}|\Phi_{2}|^{q})^{\frac{2+\delta}{q}}(\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*}|\phi|)^{s_{\delta}}])^{\frac{q-2-\delta}{q}}\Big\}.

One more application of Hölder’s inequality, with
\eta>0
such that
s_{\delta}(1+\eta)<r/\Lambda
, yields

\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*}|\phi|)^{s_{\delta}}]\leq(\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*})^{s_{\delta}(1+\eta)}])^{1/(1+\eta)}(\mathbb{E}_{P}|\phi|^{s_{\delta}(1+\eta)/\eta})^{\eta/(1+\eta)}.

The last two displays, taken together, imply that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[|\psi^{\mathrm{SR}}(D;P)|^{2+\delta}\big]\leq M<\infty,

for a constant
M
independent of
P
.
Combining this with
\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)\geq v_{0}>0
, we deduce from (
C.43
) that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\,\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}(D;P)]}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\;\leq\;\frac{C_{\delta}M}{v_{0}^{(2+\delta)/2}\,n^{\delta/2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{as }n\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

(C.45)

To finish the proof, we apply (
C.42
) and triangle inequality.
For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
P\in\mathcal{P}
,

\displaystyle\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}]}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\,+\,\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[\psi^{\,\mathtt{SR}}]}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)\right|

(C.46)

\displaystyle=:\Delta_{n,1}(t,P)+\Delta_{n,2}(t,P).

Note that (
C.45
) implies

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t}\Delta_{n,1}(t,P)\to 0
.
For the other term, we use (
C.42
) and

\inf_{P}\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)\geq v_{0}>0
to deduce, for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Delta_{n,2}(t,P)\;\leq\;\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,|r_{n}(P)|}{\sqrt{v_{0}}}>\varepsilon\right)+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl[\Phi(t+\varepsilon)-\Phi(t-\varepsilon)\bigr]\;\to\;C^{\prime}\varepsilon,

as
n\to\infty
, using
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sqrt{n}\,|r_{n}(P)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0

and the continuity of
\Phi
.
Since
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, taking
\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

in (
C.46
) and sending
\varepsilon\to 0
gives

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\,\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\,(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{SR}}_{bc}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P))}{\sqrt{\mathrm{V}_{\mathtt{SR}}(P)}}\leq t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|=0,

as desired to show.

\square

Appendix D
Supporting results

Lemma D.1

(Rate of
\widehat{g}
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
, it holds that

\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Proof of
Lemma

D.1
.

The proof simply follows using triangle inequality, since we assume that
\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
for each
k=1,\dots,K
.

\square

Lemma D.2

(Rate of
\widehat{\lambda}
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
, the empirical optimization problem (
7
) has a unique, finite minimizer
\widehat{\lambda}
with probability tending to
1
. Moreover,
\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})+O_{p}(\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})})=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\widehat{\lambda}:=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\lambda}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)
and
{\lambda}^{*}:=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\lambda}{L}(\lambda)
where

\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda):=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda\right)\right],\quad L(\lambda)=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\exp\left(g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda\right)\right].

Condition (iii) of Theorem 3.2 states that
g
and
m_{1}
have finite MGF in a radius
r>2q^{\prime}\|\lambda^{*}\|
around the origin, where
q^{\prime}=q/(q-2)
, and the same holds for
\widehat{g}
in the conditional sense:
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(r\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|)\mid\widehat{g}(\cdot)]=O_{p}(1)
. Since
\widehat{g}(\cdot)
is fitted independent of the data, the conditional Markov
inequality and the Markov inequality give

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\left(r\|g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|\right)+\exp\left(r\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|\right)\right]=O_{p}(1).

(D.1)

Throughout this proof, we fix
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
2(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})<r
. Define

A:=\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{-(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|}g(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}].

Note that
Assumption

3
(i) implies that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[gg^{\top}]
is finite and positive definite: Finiteness follows from the finiteness of the moment generating function of
g
, and if there exists
v\neq 0
such that
0=v^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{P}[gg^{\top}]v=\mathbb{E}_{P}[(v^{\top}g)^{2}]
then
v^{\top}g=0
a.s., violating
Assumption

3
(i). This, in turn, implies that
A\succ 0
because if
v\neq 0
is such that
v^{\top}Av=0
, then using that the exponential function is strictly positive, we have
g^{\top}v=0
a.s., which contradicts
\mathbb{E}_{P}[gg^{\top}]\succ 0
. Throughout, we denote by
\kappa>0
the smallest eigenvalue of
A
.

First, we prove that with probability tending to one,
\widehat{L}_{n}
has a unique minimizer
\widehat{\lambda}
and
\widehat{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lambda^{*}
. We claim that, for any
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and with
B_{\varepsilon}:=\{\lambda:\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|\,\leq\,\varepsilon\}
,

1.

\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)|=o_{p}(1)
,

2.

L(\lambda)\geq L(\lambda^{*})+\kappa\varepsilon^{2}/2
holds for every
\lambda
on the boundary
\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|=\varepsilon
.

3.

\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)\succeq A
for every
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}
, and
\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)\|=o_{p}(1)
. Consequently, with probability tending to one,
\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)\succeq(\kappa/2)I
for every
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}
.

Using the above, we can show
\widehat{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lambda^{*}
as follows. For any
\lambda^{\prime}
such that
\|\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda^{*}\|=\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{\prime})\geq L(\lambda^{\prime})-\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)|

\displaystyle\geq L(\lambda^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\varepsilon^{2}-\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)|

(using
Item

2
)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\varepsilon^{2}-2\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)|.

This in conjunction with
Item

1
implies that the event

E_{n,\varepsilon}:=\left\{\inf_{\lambda\,:\,\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|\,=\,\varepsilon}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)>\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*})\right\}

has probability tending to one.
The empirical objective
\widehat{L}_{n}
is a finite sum of exponentials, hence continuous and convex. Therefore, on the event
E_{n,\varepsilon}
, its minimum over the compact set
B_{\varepsilon}
is attained at an interior point. Since a local minimizer of a convex function is a global minimizer, we conclude that, on the event
E_{n,\varepsilon}
,
\widehat{L}_{n}
has a global minimizer in the interior of
B_{\varepsilon}
.
On the event in
Item

3
,
\widehat{L}_{n}
is strictly convex on
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
hence the minimizer is unique.

Thus, with probability tending to one,
\widehat{\lambda}
is well defined, unique, and satisfies
\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|<\varepsilon
.
Since
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
is arbitrary, the above proves the consistency. It remains to show
Items

1
,
2
and
3
.

1.

Proof of
Item

1
: First, set
\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)=\mathbb{P}_{n}[\exp(g(X_{S})^{\top}\lambda)]
and write

\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)=\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)+\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda).

The conclusion
\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda)|=o_{p}(1)
follows from
Andersen and Gill (1982, Theorem II.1)
; which applies here because
\widetilde{L}_{n}
is convex and converges pointwise (by the law of large numbers) to the finite
function
L
on the open ball of radius
r
around the origin, which contains the compact set
B_{\varepsilon}
.
To show that
\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)|=o_{p}(1)
, we rely on the following Taylor expansion:

e^{\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda}-e^{g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda}=\lambda^{\top}(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))e^{\Xi(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda},

(D.2)

for some
\Xi(X_{S_{\cap}})
that lies between
g(X_{S_{\cap}})
and
\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})
. For
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}
, we have
\|\lambda\|\leq\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon
and
\|\Xi\|\leq\max\{\|g\|,\|\widehat{g}\|\}
, hence

\exp(\lambda^{\top}\Xi)\leq\exp((\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon)\|g\|)+\exp((\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon)\|\widehat{g}\|)

(D.3)

We therefore deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)|=\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[e^{\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda}-e^{g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[|\lambda^{\top}(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))|\cdot e^{\lambda^{\top}\Xi(X_{S_{\cap}})}\right]\right|

(using (
D.2
) and triangle inequality)

\displaystyle\leq(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon)\left\|\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\right\|_{2}\left\|e^{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon)\|g\|_{2}}+e^{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon)\|\widehat{g}\|_{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

(using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
D.3
))

The above bound is
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
, since
\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
from
Lemma

D.1
, and the last factor is
O_{p}(1)
by (
D.1
).

\square

2.

Proof of
Item

2
: Applying the dominated convergence theorem with the integrable envelope
e^{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|}(1+\|g\|)^{2}
(this is integrable since
\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0}<r
), we can say that
L
is twice continuously differentiable on
B_{\varepsilon}
, with

\nabla L(\lambda)=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(g^{\top}\lambda)g],\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(g^{\top}\lambda)gg^{\top}].

It follows from a Taylor expansion of
L(\lambda)
around
\lambda^{*}
that

L(\lambda)=L(\lambda^{*})+\underbrace{\nabla L(\lambda^{*})^{\top}}_{=\,0}(\lambda-\lambda^{*})+\frac{1}{2}(\lambda-\lambda^{*})^{\top}\nabla^{2}L(\xi)(\lambda-\lambda^{*}),

for some
\xi
on the segment joining
\lambda^{*}
and
\lambda
. In the last display we used
\nabla L(\lambda^{*})=0
from the proof of
Lemma

3.1
in
Section

C.2
. Since
\|\xi-\lambda^{*}\|\leq\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|\leq\varepsilon
, we have
\|\xi\|\leq\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0}
, and thus
\exp(\xi^{\top}g)\geq\exp(-(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|)
(pointwise). This implies that
\nabla^{2}L(\xi)\succeq A
, where
A:=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(-(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|)\,gg^{\top}]\succeq\kappa I
as shown earlier.
Consequently,

L(\lambda)-L(\lambda^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}(\lambda-\lambda^{*})^{\top}A(\lambda-\lambda^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}\kappa\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa\varepsilon^{2},

as desired to show.
\square

3.

Proof of
Item

3
: The bound
\nabla^{2}{L}(\lambda)\succeq{A}
on
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
follows exactly as in the proof of
Item

2
above. For the uniform convergence, we start by writing that

\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)=\left(\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)\right)+\left(\nabla^{2}\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)\right).

(D.4)

We will show that each of the above differences is
o_{p}(1)
. For
\|\lambda\|\leq\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0}
, the derivative of the map
v\mapsto\exp(v^{\top}\lambda)\,vv^{\top}
in a direction
h
equals

\exp(v^{\top}\lambda)((h^{\top}\lambda)\,vv^{\top}+vh^{\top}+hv^{\top}),

and the norm of the above is bounded by
c(1+\|v\|)^{2}\exp\{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|v\|\}\|h\|
for a constant
c\in(0,\infty)
.
Therefore, by the mean value theorem and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)\right\|\lesssim\left\|(1+\|g\|\vee\|\widehat{g}\|)^{2}e^{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|\vee\|\widehat{g}\|}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

The first factor above is
O_{p}(1)
by (
D.1
) and the second factor is
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
by
Lemma

D.1
.

Finally, the conclusion for the second term in (
D.4
) follows from a uniform law of large numbers
(
Newey and McFadden, 1994
, Lemma 2.4)
. Indeed, observe that
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
is compact, the map
\lambda\mapsto\exp(g^{\top}\lambda)\,gg^{\top}
is continuous with an integrable envelope
\exp\{(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|\}\|g\|^{2}
. Therefore,
Newey and McFadden (1994, Lemma 2.4)
applies and yields that

\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}}\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}{L}(\lambda)\right\|=o_{p}(1).

Finally, for every
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
,
\lambda_{\min}(\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda))\geq\lambda_{\min}(\nabla^{2}L(\lambda))-\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda)\|\geq\kappa-o_{p}(1)
uniformly in
\lambda
, which gives
\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)\succeq(\kappa/2)I
on
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
with probability tending to one.
This finishes the proof of
Item

3
and thus of the consistency
\widehat{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lambda^{*}
.

\square

We next show that
\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. We work on the event where
\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and the conclusion of
Item

3
holds; we have shown above that this event has probability tending to one.
Since
\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}(\widehat{\lambda})=0
and the segment between
\lambda^{*}
and
\widehat{\lambda}
lies in
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}
, the integral form of Taylor’s theorem gives

0=\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}(\widehat{\lambda})=\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}\left(\lambda^{*}\right)+\overline{H}_{n}\cdot(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}),\quad\overline{H}_{n}:=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*}+s(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}))\,ds\succeq\frac{\kappa}{2}I.

Rearranging, we get

\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|\leq({2}/{\kappa})\|\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*})\|
.
Note that

\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}\left(\lambda^{*}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\big(g^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)g\right]+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\big(\widehat{g}^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)\widehat{g}-\exp\big(g^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)g\right].

The first term above is
O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
since
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp({g}^{\top}\lambda^{*}){g}]=0
and the variance is finite as
r>2\|\lambda^{*}\|
.
For the second term, we can show that the derivative of the map
v\mapsto\exp(v^{\top}\lambda^{*})v
has norm at most
(\|\lambda^{*}\|+1)(1+\|v\|)\exp(\|\lambda^{*}\|\|v\|)
. Therefore, using the mean value theorem and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can bound the second term above as

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\left(\widehat{g}^{\top}\lambda^{*}\right)\widehat{g}-\exp\left(g^{\top}\lambda^{*}\right)g\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq(\|\lambda^{*}\|+1)\left\|(1+\|g\|\vee\|\widehat{g}\|)\exp\left\{\|\lambda^{*}\|\|g\|\vee\|\widehat{g}\|\right\}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

Again, the last factor above is
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
by
Lemma

D.1
, and the rest is
O_{p}(1)
as in (
D.1
).
Consequently,
\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*})=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
and hence
\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

\square

Lemma D.3

(Rate of estimated transfer weights
\widehat{w}
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
,

\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1),

for any
t\in[1,\,r/\|\lambda^{*}\|)
, with the convention
r/\|\lambda^{*}\|=\infty
if
\lambda^{*}=0
. In particular, the last claim holds for
t=2q^{\prime}
where
q^{\prime}=q/(q-2)
, because
r>2q^{\prime}\|\lambda^{*}\|
by assumption in
Theorem

3.2
.

Proof.

Throughout, we use the shorthand
\Delta_{n}(x):=\widehat{g}(x)^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}-g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}
for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{K-1}
.
We first record moment bounds that are used repeatedly below. Fix
t\in[1,r/\|\lambda^{*}\|)
, and choose
\varepsilon_{t}>0
such that
t(\|\lambda^{*}\|+\varepsilon_{t})\leq r
. By
Lemma

D.2
, the event
E_{n}
on which
\widehat{L}_{n}
has a unique minimizer
\widehat{\lambda}
with
\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|\leq\varepsilon_{t}
has probability tending to one. On this event
E_{n}
, we have
t\|\widehat{\lambda}\|\leq r
by triangle inequality, and therefore

e^{t\,g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}+e^{t\,\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}\leq e^{r\|g\|}+e^{r\|\widehat{g}\|}.

Moreover, if
\xi(x)
lies between
0
and
\Delta_{n}(x)
, then
g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\xi(x)
lies between
g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}
and
\widehat{g}(x)^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}
, which gives
e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\xi}\leq e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}+e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}
. Combining these two observations with
(a+b)^{t}\leq 2^{t-1}(a^{t}+b^{t})
and with (
D.1
) from the proof of
Lemma

D.2
, we conclude that on the event
E_{n}
,

\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[e^{t\,(g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\xi(X_{S_{\cap}}))}\Big]\leq 2^{t-1}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{r\|g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|}+e^{r\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|}\big]=O_{p}(1),

(D.5)

for every
\xi
in
[0,\Delta_{n}]
(or
[\Delta_{n},0]
if
\Delta_{n}<0
). Note that
\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]\leq\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}0}]=1
by definition. Using Jensen’s inequality and the inequality
u\leq e^{ru}/r
for
u\geq 0
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}\big]\geq\exp\big(\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbb{P}_{n}[\widehat{g}]\big)\geq\exp\big(-\|\widehat{\lambda}\|\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\|\widehat{g}\|\big)\geq\exp\big(-r^{-1}\,\|\widehat{\lambda}\|\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{r\|\widehat{g}\|}]\big).

Using
\|\widehat{\lambda}\|=O_{p}(1)
and (
D.1
) we conclude that

1\big/\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}\big]=O_{p}(1).

(D.6)

The second claim of this lemma now follows from the triangle inequality, because

\|\widehat{w}\|_{L^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=\frac{(\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{t\,\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}])^{1/t}}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]},\qquad\|w^{*}\|_{L^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=\frac{(\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{t\,g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}])^{1/t}}{\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})},

and both are
O_{p}(1)
by (
D.5
) and (
D.6
). We now turn to the first claim. By Taylor expansion, there exists a (point-dependent)
\xi(x)
lying between
0
and
\Delta_{n}(x)
such that

e^{\widehat{g}(x)^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}-e^{g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}}=e^{g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\Delta_{n}(x)+R_{n}(x),\quad\text{where}\quad R_{n}(x):=\frac{1}{2}e^{g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\xi(x)}\Delta_{n}(x)^{2}.

(D.7)

First, we control the remainder term, showing it is asymptotically negligible. Applying Hölder’s inequality with
1/q^{\prime}+2/q=1
, we deduce that

\mathbb{P}_{n}[R_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})]\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\big\|e^{g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\xi(X_{S_{\cap}})}\big\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\,\|\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|^{2}_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

(D.8)

The first factor above is
O_{p}(1)
by (
D.5
) with
t=q^{\prime}
. For the second factor, note that

\|\Delta_{n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}\|\,\|g\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}+\|\widehat{\lambda}\|\,\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),

by
Lemmas

D.1
and
D.2
. Therefore, we can conclude from (
D.8
) that
\mathbb{P}_{n}[R_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

Next, we control the first-order term in (
D.7
). Write

\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\Delta_{n}\big]=(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*})^{\top}\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g\big]+\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}(\widehat{g}-g)\big],

Here
\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g]
is
O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by Chebyshev’s inequality, since
\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g]=0
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{2g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\|g\|^{2}]<\infty
. The second term is bounded above (in absolute value) by the following:

\|\widehat{\lambda}\|\,\|e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Combining with (
D.7
) and the bound on the remainder, we obtain

\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}-e^{g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\big]=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

(D.9)

We now turn to the main proof, showing that
\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. The triangle inequality gives

\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\frac{\|e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}-e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]}+\frac{|\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}]-\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]|}{\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]}\,\|w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

(D.10)

For the second term in the last display,
\|w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1)
by (
D.5
), the denominator is handled by (
D.6
), and

\big|\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}]\big|\leq\big|\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}-e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}]\big|+\big|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}]\big|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})+O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

using (
D.9
) and Chebyshev’s inequality. For the first term in (
D.10
), a first-order Taylor expansion gives

e^{\widehat{g}(x)^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}-e^{g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}}=e^{g(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\eta(x)}\Delta_{n}(x),

for some
\eta(x)
lying between
0
and
\Delta_{n}(x)
. Applying Hölder’s inequality with
1/2=1/(2q^{\prime})+1/q
,

\big\|e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\eta}\Delta_{n}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\big\|e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}+\eta}\big\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\,\|\Delta_{n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1)\,o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),

where we used (
D.5
) with
t=2q^{\prime}
and the bound on
\|\Delta_{n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
derived above. Therefore, both terms in (
D.10
) are
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. This, together with (
D.6
), completes the proof.

\square

Lemma D.4

(Rate of the affine weights
\widehat{\nu}
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
, the matrix
\mathbb{P}_{n}[\widehat{w}\,\widehat{g}\,\widehat{g}^{\top}]
is invertible with probability tending to one, and it holds that
\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
, where

\nu_{2:K}^{*}=\left(\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right],

and
\nu^{*}_{1}=1-\sum_{k=2}^{K}\nu^{*}_{k}
.

Proof.

Recall that we compute
\widehat{\nu}
as
\widehat{\nu}_{1}:=1-\sum_{k=2}^{K}\widehat{\nu}_{k}
and

\widehat{\nu}_{2:K}:=\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right].

Note that

\|\widehat{\nu}_{2:K}-\nu^{*}_{2:K}\|_{2}\leq\|D_{n}^{-1}(C_{n}-C)\|_{2}+\|(D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1})C\|_{2}
, where

\displaystyle C_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right],\qquad D_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\right],

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right],\quad\ \ D=\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}){g}(X_{S_{\cap}}){g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\right].

Recall that

\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
by assumption. On the other hand, it follows from
Lemma

D.1
that
\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
and
Lemma

D.3
gives
\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. Combining these rate results with the algebraic identity

\hat{a}\hat{b}\hat{c}-abc=ab(\hat{c}-c)+bc(\hat{a}-a)+ca(\hat{b}-b)+(\hat{a}-a)(\hat{b}-b)c\\
+(\hat{b}-b)(\hat{c}-c)a+(\hat{c}-c)(\hat{a}-a)b+(\hat{a}-a)(\hat{b}-b)(\hat{c}-c),

(D.11)

we can show that
\|C_{n}-C\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
as well as
\|D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1}\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. For instance,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\right\|_{2}\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+|\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}|\|\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,g(X_{S_{\cap}})]\|_{2}

(by triangle inequality and Cauchy-Schwarz)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/4})+o_{p}(n^{-1/4})O_{p}(n^{-1/2})

(using (
C.5
) and finite moments)

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Similarly, using the notation
m_{1}(X_{S_{\cap}})=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(m_{1}(X_{S_{\cap}})-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|_{2}\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|_{2}

(by Cauchy-Schwarz)

\displaystyle\qquad=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/4})

(using
Lemma

D.1
and finite moments)

\displaystyle\qquad=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))g(X_{S_{\cap}})(\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{1}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|_{2}\|{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})(m_{1}(X_{S_{\cap}})-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|_{2}

(by Cauchy-Schwarz)

\displaystyle\qquad=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})O_{p}(1)

(using
Lemma

D.3
and finite moments)

\displaystyle\qquad=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

The last three displays and the identity (
D.11
) together imply that
\|C_{n}-C\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. It remains to show that
D_{n}
is invertible with probability tending to one, and
\|D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1}\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
. Write

D_{n}-D=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)[w^{*}gg^{\top}]+\mathbb{P}_{n}[(\widehat{w}-w^{*})\widehat{g}\widehat{g}^{\top}]+\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(\widehat{g}\widehat{g}^{\top}-gg^{\top})].

The first term above is
O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by Chebyshev’s inequality, because the variance is finite as
\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*})^{2}\|g\|^{4}]<\infty
which in turn follows from the finiteness of the moment generating function of
g(X_{S_{\cap}})
in a radius
r>2\|\lambda^{*}\|
around the origin. The second term is bounded by the product of
\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
which is
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
by
Lemma

D.3
, and
\|\widehat{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
which is
O_{p}(1)
due to the finiteness of the moment generating function of
\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})
conditional on
\widehat{g}(\cdot)
. Finally, for the third term, we use the inequality
\|\widehat{g}\widehat{g}^{\top}-gg^{\top}\|\leq(\|\widehat{g}\|+\|g\|)\|\widehat{g}-g\|
and Hölder’s inequality with
1/(2q^{\prime})+1/q=1/2
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(\widehat{g}\widehat{g}^{\top}-gg^{\top})]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|w^{*}(\|\widehat{g}\|+\|g\|)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|w^{*}\right\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|\|\widehat{g}\|+\|g\|\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|\widehat{g}-g\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

(using
Lemma

D.1
)

Taken together, the above bounds tell us that
D_{n}-D=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

Next, write
\lambda_{\min}(M)
for the smallest eigenvalue of a matrix
M
. Observe that
\lambda_{\min}(D)>0
because for any
u\neq 0
we have
P(u^{\top}g\neq 0)>0
and
w^{*}
, which imply that
u^{\top}Du=\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}(u^{\top}g)^{2}]>0
. Since
\lambda_{\min}(D_{n})\geq\lambda_{\min}(D)-\|D_{n}-D\|_{2}
and
\|D_{n}-D\|_{2}=o_{p}(1)
, the event
\{\lambda_{\min}(D_{n})>\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}(D)\}
has probability tending to one. On this event,
D_{n}
is invertible with
\|D_{n}^{-1}\|_{2}\leq 1/\lambda_{\min}(D_{n})\leq 2/\lambda_{\min}(D)
. Therefore, using the identity
D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1}=D_{n}^{-1}(D-D_{n})D^{-1}
, we deduce that

\left\|D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{\lambda_{\min}(D)^{2}}\|D_{n}-D\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

The above, together with
\|C_{n}-C\|_{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
and the fact that
\lambda_{\min}(D_{n})>\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}(D)>0
holds with probability tending to one, gives us the desired conclusion, since
\|\widehat{\nu}_{2:K}-\nu^{*}_{2:K}\|_{2}\leq\|D_{n}^{-1}(C_{n}-C)\|_{2}+\|(D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1})C\|_{2}
.

\square

Lemma D.5

(AIPW approximation)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\right)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\right)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

Define
\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)
as in
Algorithm

1
and
\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)
as in
Theorem

3.2
. We can write

\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)=\sum_{l=1}^{3}(\Delta_{l,1}(D)+\Delta_{l,0}(D)),

(D.12)

where
D=(Y,A,X)
, and

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,1}(D)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)A(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}(D)

\displaystyle:=\left(1-\frac{A}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)(\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{S_{k}})-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))

\displaystyle\Delta_{3,1}(D)

\displaystyle:=-A\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)(\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{S_{k}})-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})),

and
\Delta_{l,0}(D)
are defined in an analogous fashion. It follows from the tower property (and that
\widehat{e}
is externally fitted / using cross-fitting) that

\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)A(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))\;\Big|\;\widehat{e}(\cdot),X_{S_{k}}\right]=0,

since
\widehat{e}(\cdot)
is independent of the data and
\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[A\left(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})\right)\mid X_{S_{k}}\right]=0
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)A(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))\right]\;\Big|\;\widehat{e}(\cdot),\,X_{1,\,S_{k}},\dots,\,X_{n,\,S_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(w^{*}(X_{i,\,S_{\cap}}))^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{i,S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i,S_{k}})}\right)^{2}{\rm Var}(Y_{i}\mid A_{i}=1,X_{i,\,S_{k}})

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))^{2}(\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}}))^{2}\right]

(using
{\rm Var}(Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{k}})\leq C
and strong overlap)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\,\|(w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))^{2}\|_{L^{q/(q-2)}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|(\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}}))^{2}\|_{L^{q/2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

(Using Hölder’s inequality with
1/{q^{\prime}}+2/q=1
)

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\,\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}\|\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1}),

The last rate is due to the facts that
\|\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(1)
, and
\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1)
which follows from the assumption that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(r\|g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|)]<\infty
for
r\geq 2q^{\prime}\|\lambda^{*}\|
.
The above display combined with Chebyshev’s inequality tell us that

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{1,1}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

We can follow a similar path to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{1,0}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{2,0}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{2,1}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Showing
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{3,1}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
is slightly different. Note that by Hölder’s inequality, with
q^{\prime}>1
such that
1/q^{\prime}+2/q=1
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{3,1}(D)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{S_{k}})-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where the second stochastic order follows from the assumption on the product of rates, and the first one follows from the assumption that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(r\|g(X_{S_{\cap}})\|)]<\infty
for
r\geq q^{\prime}\|\lambda^{*}\|
.
The proof of
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\Delta_{3,0}(D)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
is analogous to the above. This concludes the proof of the first claim, i.e., that

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\right)\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

To show the second claim, we use
Lemma

D.3
to say that

\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1),

where
q^{\prime}=q/(q-2)
. We then use the decomposition (
D.12
) to write

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\left(\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\right)\right]\\
=\sum_{l=1}^{3}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-{w}^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)(\Delta_{l,1}+\Delta_{l,0})\right].

We handle the first two terms in the same manner as earlier, since they constitute of summands that have conditional mean zero. The key observation is that

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\Delta_{1,1}\right]\;\Big|\;\widehat{e},\,\widehat{\lambda},\,\widehat{g},\,X_{1,\,S_{k}},\dots,\,X_{n,\,S_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle={\rm Var}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}-w^{*}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right)A(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))\right]\;\Big|\;\widehat{e},\,\widehat{\lambda},\,\widehat{g},\,X_{1,\,S_{k}},\dots,\,X_{n,\,S_{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{e}(X_{i,S_{k}})}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i,S_{k}})}\right)^{2}{\rm Var}(Y_{i}\mid A_{i}=1,X_{i,\,S_{k}})

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)^{2}\left(\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}})\right)^{2}\right]

(using
{\rm Var}(Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{k}})\leq C
and strong overlap)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}\|\widehat{e}\,(X_{S_{k}})-e(X_{S_{k}})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}

(Hölder’s inequality)

\displaystyle=n^{-1}O_{p}(1)o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(n^{-1}),

and likewise for
\Delta_{1,0}
,
\Delta_{2,1}
and
\Delta_{2,0}
. For the remaining part we directly apply Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\Delta_{3,1}(D)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|{\widehat{e}(X_{S_{k}})}-{e(X_{S_{k}})}\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\left\|\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{S_{k}})-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and likewise for
\Delta_{3,0}
. This completes the proof.

\square

Lemma D.6

(Taylor expansion of transfer weights)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\,\widehat{\lambda}-g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}^{*}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
.

Proof.

First we observe that
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, and therefore

\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]\right|\\
\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\nu^{*}_{k}|\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Consequently,
\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
, and thus

\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[e^{\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[e^{g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}}\right]}-1\right)\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{patch:D3-first}}}{{=}}O_{p}(n^{-1/4})o_{p}(n^{-1/4})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]-\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\,\widehat{\lambda}-g(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\lambda^{*}\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(e^{\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})}-1-\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\Delta_{n}(x):=\widehat{g}(x)^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}-{g}(x)^{\top}\lambda^{*}
. It follows from the proof of
Lemma

D.3
that
\|\Delta_{n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Consequently, using the Taylor expansion (
D.7
) from the proof of
Lemma

D.3
, we deduce the following.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(e^{\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})}-1-\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\xi(X_{S_{\cap}})}\Delta_{n}^{2}(X_{S_{\cap}})w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{\xi(X_{S_{\cap}})}w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\Delta_{n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}

(by Hölder’s inequality)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1/2})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where
1/q^{\prime}+2/q=1
. To see why the first factor in the above bound is
O_{p}(1)
, note that
w^{*}e^{\Delta_{n}}=e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}/\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})=\widehat{w}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{\widehat{g}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}}]/\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})\leq\widehat{w}/\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})
on the event of
Lemma

D.2
. Therefore, by another application of Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\big\|e^{\xi(X_{S_{\cap}})}w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\big\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\big\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}+\|e^{\Delta_{n}(X_{S_{\cap}})}w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\big\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

(using
e^{\xi}\leq 1+e^{\Delta_{n}}
)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\|w^{*}\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}+\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})^{-1}\|\widehat{w}\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\Big)\|\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1),

using
Lemma

D.3
.
This completes the proof.

\square

Lemma D.7

(An approximation of the bias-correction term)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.2
, the bias-correction term
B_{n}
in
Algorithm

1
is asymptotically equivalent to the following

\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}}))\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right],

in the sense that
B_{n}=\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}+o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
, where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}^{*}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}
.

Proof.

Recall that we define the bias-correction term
B_{n}
in
Algorithm

1
as

B_{n}:=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})\left(\Delta\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{\nu}_{k}\,\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\left[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}\right]-\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right],

where
\Delta\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X):=\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{1}(Y,A,X)-\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)
, and other quantities are as defined in
Algorithm

1
. Also define

{B}_{n}^{*}=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-{g}(X_{S_{\cap}}))\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right],

where
\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)=\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{1}(Y,A,X)-\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)
, and recall that
g(X_{S_{\cap}})=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\Delta\tau(X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
.
Lemma

D.16
yields
{\rm Var}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq C
(for some constant) and thus
Lemma

D.15
tells us that

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

provided we show that

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))^{2}\cdot\phi^{2}(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\,\right]=o_{p}(1).

(D.13)

Applying Hölder’s inequality with exponents
s,s^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
,
1/s+1/s^{\prime}=1
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}}))^{2}\cdot\phi^{2}(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\,\right]\right|\leq\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2s}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}\|\phi\|_{L^{2s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}.

The second factor is
O_{p}(1)
for any
s^{\prime}>1
, because
m_{1}
and
g
have finite moment generating function in an open neighborhood of zero. For the first factor, pick
t\in(2,r/\|\lambda^{*}\|)
(which is possible to do, because
r\geq 2q^{\prime}\|\lambda^{*}\|>2\|\lambda^{*}\|
) and pick
2<2s<t
. Write
1/(2s)=\theta/2+(1-\theta)/t
. Then, by the interpolation inequality for
L^{p}
norms (which again follows from Hölder’s inequality),

\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2s}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{\theta}\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{1-\theta}.

The first factor above is
o_{p}(1)
by
Lemma

D.3
, and the second factor is
O_{p}(1)
due to finite moment generating functions of
g
and
\widehat{g}
. This finishes the proof of (
D.13
), and thus proves via
Lemma

D.15
that
T_{1}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

On the other hand, it follows by an argument similar to our proof of
Lemma

D.5
(using Hölder’s inequality) that

T_{2}:=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\Delta\widehat{\tau}^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X))\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Next, define

T_{3}:=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))\Delta_{f}(X_{S_{\cap}})\right],

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}(X_{S_{\cap}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{\nu}_{k}\underbrace{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\left[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}\right]}_{\widehat{m}_{k}}-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu^{*}_{k}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}\right]}_{m_{k}}-(\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{\nu}_{k}(\widehat{m}_{k}-m_{k})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\widehat{\nu}_{k}-\nu_{k}^{*})m_{k}-(\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}).

This gives

T_{3}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widehat{\nu}_{k}\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g)(\widehat{m}_{k}-m_{k})]\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\widehat{\nu}_{k}-\nu_{k}^{*})\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g)m_{k}]\\
-(\widehat{\tau}_{1}^{\mathtt{R}}-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})\,\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-g)].

Applying
Lemma

D.4
and (
C.5
), we can conclude that the last two sums are
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. For the first sum, we can apply
Lemma

D.15
, because by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[(w^{*})^{2}(\widehat{m}_{k}-m_{k})^{2}\right]\leq\|w^{*}\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}\,\|\widehat{m}_{k}-m_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1),

and
Lemma

D.16
gives
{\rm Var}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq C
for some finite constant
C
.
Therefore
Lemma

D.15
applies and yields that
T_{3}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
.

Finally, define

T_{2}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu^{*}_{k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}\right]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}\right)\right].

We can use the algebraic identity
\hat{a}\hat{b}\hat{c}-abc=ab(\hat{c}-c)+bc(\hat{a}-a)+ca(\hat{b}-b)+(\hat{a}-a)(\hat{b}-b)c+(\hat{b}-b)(\hat{c}-c)a+(\hat{c}-c)(\hat{a}-a)b+(\hat{a}-a)(\hat{b}-b)(\hat{c}-c)
to write

B_{n}-B_{n}^{*}=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}(T_{1}+T_{2}-T_{2}^{\prime}+T_{3})+o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

On the other hand,

B_{n}^{*}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}}))\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*})\right]=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}T_{2}^{\prime}.

Therefore, using
Lemma

D.2
and the fact that
T_{1}
,
T_{2}
and
T_{3}
are all
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
to conclude that

\displaystyle B_{n}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=B_{n}-B_{n}^{*}+B_{n}^{*}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{*})^{\top}(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3})+o_{p}(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3})

\displaystyle=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

as desired to show.

\square

Lemma D.8

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.1
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, it holds that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})-g(X_{S_{\cap}};\,P)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Proof.

Write
\widehat{m}_{k}(X_{S_{\cap}}):=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\widehat{\tau}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
, and
m_{k}(X_{S_{\cap}}):={\mathbb{E}}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
for each
k
. Then,
\widehat{g}_{k}=\widehat{m}_{1}-\widehat{m}_{k}
by construction and
g_{k}=m_{1}-m_{k}
by definition. Using the triangle inequality,

\|\widehat{g}_{k}-g_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\leq\|\widehat{m}_{1}-m_{1}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}+\|\widehat{m}_{k}-m_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})},

and both the above terms are
o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
uniformly in
P\in\mathcal{P}
, by assumption.

\square

Lemma D.9

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.2
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, it holds that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}(P)\|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Proof.

We follow the proof of
Lemma

D.2
closely and make every bound uniform in
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Set
\Lambda=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\lambda^{*}(P)\|
, which is finite by
Lemma

B.6
. Throughout, we fix
\varepsilon_{0}>0
with
2(\Lambda+2\varepsilon_{0})<r
, where
r
is the radius as in
Theorem

B.7
. For
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, denote
B_{\varepsilon}(P):=\{\lambda:\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}(P)\|\leq\varepsilon\}
. The triangle inequality yields
\|\lambda\|\leq\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0}
for
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)
.

Using Markov’s inequality and condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
,

\lim_{M\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\Big(\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[e^{r\|g(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\|}+e^{r\|\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})\|}\big]>M\Big)=0,

(D.14)

which is the uniform version of (
D.1
).

Next, consider the matrix

A(P):=\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|}gg^{\top}].

As in the proof of
Lemma

D.2
, we will show that
A(P)\succeq\kappa I
for some
\kappa>0
; the additional challenge here is to find a choice of
\kappa>0
that works uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Towards this, we write

G:=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{r\|g\|}]<\infty
. With
c_{0}
and
\zeta
as in
Assumption

B.3
, choose
M_{0}>0
large enough such that
e^{-rM_{0}}G\leq c_{0}/2
. We will show that the choice
\kappa:=\frac{1}{2}c_{0}\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}
fits the bill.
For a unit vector
u
, we apply Markov’s inequality and use
Assumption

B.3
to deduce that

\displaystyle u^{\top}A(P)u

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}\,P\big(u^{\top}g\geq\zeta,\ \|g\|\leq M_{0}\big)

(Markov’s inequality)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}\big(P(u^{\top}g\geq\zeta)-P(\|g\|>M_{0})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}\big(c_{0}-P(\|g\|>M_{0})\big)

(by
Assumption

B.3
)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}\big(c_{0}-e^{-rM_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{r\|g\|}]\big)

(Markov’s inequality)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta^{2}e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})M_{0}}\big(c_{0}-e^{-rM_{0}}G\big)\geq\kappa.

(by definition)

This shows that
A(P)\succeq\kappa I
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Then, as in the proof of
Item

2
of
Lemma

D.2
,
\nabla L(\lambda;P)=0
at
\lambda=\lambda^{*}(P)
and
\nabla^{2}L(\lambda;P)\succeq A(P)\succeq\kappa I
for
\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)
.

We next state the uniform versions of
Items

1
,
2
and
3
from the proof of
Lemma

D.2
: For any
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
,

1.

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon}(P)}|\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-L(\lambda;P)|=o_{p}(1)
;

2.

L(\lambda;P)\geq L(\lambda^{*}(P);P)+\kappa\varepsilon^{2}/2
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
and every
\lambda
with
\|\lambda-\lambda^{*}(P)\|=\varepsilon
;

3.

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)}\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda;P)\|=o_{p}(1)
; consequently,

\inf_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\big(\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)\succeq(\kappa/2)I\text{ for all }\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)\big)\to 1.

Given
Items

1
,
2
and
3
, the consistency argument in the proof of
Lemma

D.2
applies verbatim with every probability replaced by its infimum over
P\in\mathcal{P}
, and yields events
E_{n}(P)
with
\inf_{P}P(E_{n}(P))\to 1
such that, on the event
E_{n}(P)
the minimizer
\widehat{\lambda}
exists, is unique, and satisfies
\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}(P)\|<\varepsilon
.
