The proof of
Item

2
closely follows the exact steps in the proof of
Item

2
in the proof of
Item

2
of
Lemma

D.2
, thus omitted. We next show
Items

1
and
3
.
To do this in one go, write

\widehat{L}_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}[F_{0}(\cdot,\widehat{g})],\qquad\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{n}[F_{2}(\cdot,\widehat{g})],

i.e.,
F_{0}(\lambda,x)=\exp(x^{\top}\lambda)
and
F_{2}(\lambda,x)=\exp(x^{\top}\lambda)\,xx^{\top}
.
We decompose
\mathbb{P}_{n}[F_{j}(\lambda,\widehat{g})]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[F_{j}(\lambda,g)]
into a plug-in part and a sampling part:

\mathbb{P}_{n}[F_{j}(\lambda,\widehat{g})]-\mathbb{E}_{P}[F_{j}(\lambda,g)]=\mathbb{P}_{n}[F_{j}(\lambda,\widehat{g})-F_{j}(\lambda,g)]+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})[F_{j}(\lambda,g)]

The bounds for the plug-in part in the proofs of
Items

1
and
3
of
Lemma

D.2
extend verbatim to this case, i.e., hold uniformly in
P
over
\mathcal{P}
. This is because those involved only
\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
which holds uniformly by
Lemma

D.8
, and
\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{r\|g\|}+e^{r\|\widehat{g}\|}]=O_{p}(1)
which holds uniformly by (
D.14
). Next, for the sampling part, note that for
\|\lambda\|,\|\lambda^{\prime}\|\leq\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0}
, we have the Lipschitz bound

\|F_{j}(\lambda,x)-F_{j}(\lambda^{\prime},x)\|\leq\|x\|^{j+1}e^{(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|x\|}\|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\|.

Fix
\delta>0
, and let
N_{\delta}(P)
be a
\delta
-net of
B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)
with at most
N_{\delta}:=(1+2\varepsilon_{0}/\delta)^{K-1}
points (note, this covering number does not depend on
P
). Then,

\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)}\big\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})F_{j}(\lambda,g)\big\|\leq\max_{\lambda\in N_{\delta}(P)}\big\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})F_{j}(\lambda,g)\big\|+\delta\,(\mathbb{P}_{n}+\mathbb{E}_{P})\big[\|g\|^{j+1}e^{(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|}\big].

(D.15)

Since
r>2(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})
, we can write

e^{2(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|x\|}\|x\|^{2j}+\|x\|^{j+1}e^{(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|x\|}\leq Ce^{r\|x\|},

(D.16)

for each
j\in\{0,2\}
, where
C=C({r,\Lambda})\in(0,\infty)
is an absolute constant. Using (
D.16
), Chebyshev’s inequality and the union bound, we arrive at

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\Big(\max_{\lambda\in N_{\delta}(P)}\big\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})F_{j}(\lambda,g)\big\|>\eta\Big)\leq\frac{N_{\delta}CG}{n\eta^{2}}.

On the other hand, using (
D.16
) and Markov’s inequality, we get

\sup_{P}P(\delta(\mathbb{P}_{n}+\mathbb{E}_{P})[\|g\|^{j+1}e^{(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|}]>\eta)\leq 2\delta CG/\eta.

Combining the last two displays with (
D.15
), we get

\sup_{P}P\left(\sup_{\lambda\in B_{\varepsilon_{0}}(P)}\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E}_{P})F_{j}(\lambda,g)\|>2\eta\right)\leq\frac{N_{\delta}CG}{\eta^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}+\frac{2CG}{\eta}\cdot\delta.

Choosing
\delta
small first and then
n
large proves the desired uniform convergence in
Items

1
and
3
. Finally, as in
Lemma

D.2
, the smallest eigenvalue of
\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)
is at least
\kappa-\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda)-\nabla^{2}L(\lambda;P)\|
, which gives the last assertion of
Item

3
.

Finally, we bound
\|\widehat{w}-w^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.
We work with the event
E_{n}(P)
intersected with the event in
Item

3
, which holds with probability tending to one. On this event, the integral form of Taylor’s theorem gives

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}(P)\|\leq(2/\kappa)\|\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}(\lambda^{*}(P))\|,

\displaystyle\nabla\widehat{L}_{n}\left(\lambda^{*}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\big(g^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)g\right]+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\exp\big(\widehat{g}^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)\widehat{g}-\exp\big(g^{\top}\lambda^{*}\big)g\right].

exactly as in the proof of
Lemma

D.2
. The term
\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g]
is an i.i.d. sum of mean zero summands with the variance bounded by
CG
, due to (
D.16
) and the definition of
G
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}_{n}[e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g]=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
uniformly in
P
by Chebyshev’s inequality. The plug-in term
O_{p}(1)\cdot\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
can be bounded using the mean value theorem exactly as in the proof of
Lemma

D.2
, by at most
O_{p}(1)\cdot\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
uniformly in
P
, due to (
D.14
) and
\sup_{P\in\mathbb{P}}\|\widehat{g}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
(
Lemma

D.8
). This leads to the desired conclusion that
\sup_{P}\|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda^{*}(P)\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

\square

Lemma D.10

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.3
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, it holds that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),

and for every
t\in[1,r/\Lambda)
,

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\|_{L^{t}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1).

Proof.

The proof of
Lemma

D.3
uses
Lemmas

D.1
and
D.2
, the moment bound (
D.1
), Chebyshev’s inequality for averages of
e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}
and
e^{g^{\top}\lambda^{*}}g
, and the positivity of
\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})
. Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, each of these holds uniformly in
P\in\mathcal{P}
. More precisely,

•

Lemma

D.8
extends
Lemma

D.1
uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.

•

Lemma

D.9
extends
Lemma

D.2
uniformly over
\mathcal{P}
.

•

(
D.14
) replaces (
D.1
).

•

The variances in the Chebyshev bounds are now uniformly bounded by a constant (depending only on
r
, and
\Lambda
) times
\sup_{P}\mathbb{E}_{P}[e^{r\|g\|}]<\infty
.

•

\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*}(P))\geq\exp(-\Lambda\sup_{P}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|g\|)>0
by Jensen’s inequality.

Therefore, replacing
\|\lambda^{*}\|
by
\Lambda
throughout the proof of
Lemma

D.3
yields both the desired claims uniformly in
P
over
\mathcal{P}
.

\square

Lemma D.11

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.4
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, it holds that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}(P)\|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

Proof.

We start by writing, from the proof of
Lemma

D.4
, that

\widehat{\nu}_{2:K}-\nu^{*}_{2:K}=D_{n}^{-1}(C_{n}-C)+(D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1})C,

where
D=\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}gg^{\top}]
and

C=\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}g\,(m_{1}(X_{S_{\cap}})-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}})]
,
m_{k}(X_{S_{\cap}})=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
(here we suppress the additional dependence on
P
to reduce notational clutter).

The decompositions of
C_{n}-C
and
D_{n}-D
in the proof of
Lemma

D.4
and the corresponding bounds hold uniformly in
P\in\mathcal{P}
, by
Lemmas

D.8
and
D.10
and conditions (1) and (4) of
Theorem

B.7
. Thus,

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|C_{n}-C\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}),\quad{and}\quad\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|D_{n}^{-1}-D^{-1}\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4}).

The vector
C=C(P)
is uniformly bounded by condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
. Moreover,
D(P)\succeq\kappa I
uniformly in
P
, with
\kappa
as in the proof of
Lemma

D.9
. More precisely, since
\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*}(P))\leq 1
, we have
w^{*}(\cdot\,;P)\geq e^{-\Lambda\|g\|}\geq e^{-(\Lambda+\varepsilon_{0})\|g\|}
, hence
D(P)\succeq A(P)\succeq\kappa I
.
Therefore the arguments in the proof of
Lemma

D.4
, with
\lambda_{\min}(D)
replaced by
\kappa
, shows that
D_{n}
is invertible with probability tending to one uniformly in
P
, and that
\sup_{P}\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu^{*}(P)\|=o_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

\square

Lemma D.12

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.5
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, it holds
for each
k=1,\dots,K
that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\!\bigl[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\bigl(\widehat{\tau}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(D)-\tau_{k}^{\,\mathtt{AIPW}}(D;P)\bigr)\bigr]\right|\;=\;o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

and the same holds with
(\widehat{w}-w^{*})
replacing
w^{*}
.

Proof.

The proof of
Lemma

D.5
bounds each of the six terms in (
D.12
) either by the conditional Chebyshev’s inequality or by Hölder’s inequality. We check here that each of those bound holds uniformly in
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

Recall that
r>2q^{\prime}\Lambda
where
q^{\prime}=q/(q-2)
and
\Lambda=\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\lambda^{*}(P)\|<\infty
. Therefore, invoking condition (4) of
Theorem

B.7
and applying Markov’s inequality, we get

\sup_{P}\|w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\|_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=O_{p}(1),

(D.17)

and
Lemma

D.10
yields the same for
\widehat{w}-w^{*}
.

Next, we consider the terms in (
D.12
) in order.
For the term
\Delta_{1,1}
defined in the proof of
Lemma

D.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}{\rm Var}_{P}\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}\Delta_{1,1}]\mid\widehat{e},X_{1,S_{k}},\dots,X_{n,S_{k}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}C\xi^{-4}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|w^{*}\|^{2}_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\|\widehat{e}-e\|^{2}_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}

(by conditions (2) and (3) of
Theorem

B.7
)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)o_{p}(n^{-1}),

(using (
D.17
) and condition (1) of
Theorem

B.7
)

and hence the desired conclusion follows by Chebyshev’s inequality. The proof for
\Delta_{1,0}
is exactly similar.

For
\Delta_{2,a}
,
{\rm Var}(1-A/e(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{k}})\leq\xi^{-1}
gives the bound
n^{-1}\xi^{-1}\|w^{*}\|^{2}_{L^{2q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a}\|^{2}_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
. Since
\sup_{P}\|\widehat{e}-e\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
and
\sup_{P}\|\widehat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}
are
o_{p}(1)
by condition (1) of
Theorem

B.7
, the conditional Chebyshev inequality (as in the proof of
Lemma

D.15
) shows that these terms are
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
uniformly in
P
.

For
\Delta_{3,a}
, Hölder’s inequality with
1/q^{\prime}+2/q=1
and strong overlap give

|\mathbb{P}_{n}[w^{*}\Delta_{3,a}]|\leq\xi^{-2}\|w^{*}\|_{L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{e}-e\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}\|\widehat{\mu}_{a}-\mu_{a}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{P}_{n})},

which is
o_{p}(n^{-1/2})
uniformly by the product-rate part of condition (1) of
Theorem

B.7
. The same arguments apply with
\widehat{w}-w^{*}
in place of
w^{*}
, using
Lemma

D.10
.

\square

Lemma D.13

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.6
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left(\widehat{w}(X_{S_{\cap}})-w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)\left(\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}})^{\top}\,\widehat{\lambda}-g(X_{S_{\cap}};P)^{\top}\lambda^{*}(P)\right)\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right]+R_{n}^{w}(P),

where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu,P)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}};P)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)
, and the remainder term is uniformly asymptotically negligible:

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}|R_{n}^{w}(P)|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

Putting together the algebraic decompositions used in the proof of
Lemma

D.6
, the remainder terms are controlled using
Lemma

D.3
, the moment bounds from the proof of
Lemma

D.3
and Hölder’s inequality. Since
Lemma

D.3
generalizes uniformly as in
Lemma

D.10
and the moment bounds also hold uniformly under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, the rest of the proof of
Lemma

D.6
carries through uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

\square

Lemma D.14

(Uniform version of
Lemma

D.7
)
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

B.7
, the bias-correction term
B_{n}
in
Algorithm

1
is uniformly asymptotically equivalent to the following

\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}(P)=\widehat{\lambda}^{\top}\,\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}};P)(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}(Y,A,X)-\widehat{g}(X_{S_{\cap}}))\,\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu^{*}(P),P)\right],

where
\phi(X_{S_{\cap}};\nu,P):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nu_{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}};P)\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]-\overline{\tau}^{\mathtt{R}}(P)
, in the sense that

\sup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\left|B_{n}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}(P)\right|=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

The proof of
Lemma

D.7
decomposes
B_{n}-\widehat{B}_{n}^{*}
into terms

T_{1},T_{2},T_{2}^{\prime},T_{3}
, each handled by
Lemma

D.15
together with the supporting results
Lemmas

D.5
,
D.1
,
D.4
,
D.2
,
D.3
and
D.6
. Since we already have shown uniform versions of these results (namely,
Lemmas

D.9
,
D.8
,
D.12
,
D.10
,
D.11
and
D.13
),
all of the bounds in the proof of
Lemma

D.7
carry through uniformly over
P\in\mathcal{P}
, thereby completing the proof.

\square

Lemma D.15
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
\sigma
-field, and assume that
\{(U_{i},V_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent of each other and mutually independent of
\mathcal{F}
. Let
\widehat{a}(\cdot)
be such that
\widehat{a}(V_{1}),\dots,\widehat{a}(V_{n})
are measurable with respect to
\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{F}\vee\sigma(V_{1},\dots,V_{n})
(i.e.,
\widehat{a}
may depend on
\mathcal{F}
and on
V_{1},\dots,V_{n}
, but not on
U_{1},\dots,U_{n}
). Assume further that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{a}(V_{i})-a(V_{i}))^{2}\cdot{\rm Var}(U_{i}\mid V_{i})=o_{p}(1).

Then,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{a}(V_{i})-a(V_{i}))(U_{i}-\mathbb{E}\,[U_{i}\mid V_{i}])=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Two special cases are worth spelling out: (a)
{\rm Var}(U_{i}\mid V_{i})\leq C<\infty
and
\|\widehat{a}-a\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(1)
, and (b)
\{(U_{i},V_{i})\}
are i.i.d. with
\mathbb{E}[{\rm Var}(U\mid V)^{\beta^{\prime}}<\infty
and
\|\widehat{a}-a\|_{L^{2\beta}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}=o_{p}(1)
where
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
such that
1/\beta+1/\beta^{\prime}=1
.

Proof of
Lemma

D.15
.

Write
S_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{a}(V_{i})-a(V_{i}))(U_{i}-\mathbb{E}\,[U_{i}\mid V_{i}])
. Since
\{(U_{i},V_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent of each other and independent of
\mathcal{F}
, conditionally on
\mathcal{G}
the variables
U_{1},\dots,U_{n}
are independent, and each
U_{i}
has the conditional law of
U_{i}
given
V_{i}
. Then, because
\widehat{a}(V_{1}),\dots,\widehat{a}(V_{n})
are
\mathcal{G}
-measurable, it follows that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[S_{n}\mid\mathcal{G}]=0
, and

\mathbb{E}_{P}[S_{n}^{2}\mid\mathcal{G}]=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\widehat{a}(V_{i})-a(V_{i})\big)^{2}\cdot{\rm Var}(U_{i}\mid V_{i})=:\frac{R_{n}}{n}.

We have
R_{n}=o_{p}(1)
by assumption. Hence for any
\varepsilon>0
and
\eta>0
, Chebyshev’s inequality yields

\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{n}|S_{n}|>\varepsilon\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\min\{1,\,R_{n}/\varepsilon^{2}\}\right]\leq\eta/\varepsilon^{2}+\mathbb{P}\left(R_{n}>\eta\right).

Letting
n\to\infty
and then
\eta\to 0
completes the proof of the main claim.

The special case (a) is immediate. For case (b), note that Hölder’s inequality with exponents
1/\beta+1/\beta^{\prime}=1
yields

R_{n}\leq\|\widehat{a}(V_{i})-a(V_{i})\|_{L^{2\beta}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}^{2}\|{\rm Var}(U_{i}\mid V_{i})\|_{L^{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}.

The second factor above is
O_{p}(1)
by Markov’s inequality, while the first factor is
o_{p}(1)
. This proves
R_{n}=o_{p}(1)
, as desired to show.

\square

Lemma D.16

(Conditional variances and integrability)
.

Suppose that
Assumption

2
holds, and that
{\rm Var}_{P}[Y\mid A=a,X_{S_{\cap}}]\leq C
and
{\rm Var}_{P}[Y\mid A=a,X_{S_{k}}]\leq C
almost surely for
a=0,1
and
k=1,\dots,K
. Then, almost surely, for each
k=1,\dots,K
,

(a)

{\rm Var}_{P}(\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq 4C/\xi
,

(b)

{\rm Var}_{P}(\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq 6C/\xi
, and
{\rm Var}_{P}(\Delta\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq 24C/\xi
for
k\geq 2
.

If, in addition,
Assumption

3
and condition (iii) of
Theorem

3.2
hold, then

(c)

\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}(X_{S_{\cap}})|\tau(X_{S_{k}})|]<\infty
for each
k
.

Proof.

(a) Fix any nonnegative measurable function
h
of
X_{S_{k}}
. Using the tower property and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\mid X_{S_{k}}]=e(X_{S_{k}})\geq\xi
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big[\frac{A}{e(X_{S_{k}})}h(X_{S_{k}})\,\Big|\,X_{S_{\cap}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\xi^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}[A\,h(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]\leq\xi^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X_{S_{k}})\mid A=1,X_{S_{\cap}}],

where in the last step we used
\mathbb{E}_{P}[Ah(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]=P(A=1\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[h\mid A=1,X_{S_{\cap}}]
. Using the above inequality with
h:=(\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})-\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{\cap}}])^{2}
, we obtain that

{\rm Var}_{P}(\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq\xi^{-1}{\rm Var}_{P}(\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})\mid A=1,X_{S_{\cap}})\leq\xi^{-1}{\rm Var}_{P}(Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{\cap}})\leq C/\xi,

where the middle inequality follows from the law of total variance conditionally on
(A=1,X_{S_{\cap}})
, using
\mathbb{E}_{P}[Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{k}}]=\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}})
. The same argument with
1-A
in place of
A
gives
{\rm Var}_{P}(\mu_{0}(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq C/\xi
. Since
\tau=\mu_{1}-\mu_{0}
, we conclude that

{\rm Var}_{P}(\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq 2(C/\xi+C/\xi)=4C/\xi,

which proves (a).
\square

(b) Write the AIPW functional as
\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)=\tau(X_{S_{k}})+\epsilon_{k}
, where
\epsilon_{k}:=\frac{A}{e(X_{S_{k}})}(Y-\mu_{1}(X_{S_{k}}))-\frac{1-A}{1-e(X_{S_{k}})}(Y-\mu_{0}(X_{S_{k}}))
satisfies
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\epsilon_{k}\mid X_{S_{k}}]=0
. Hence, by tower property,
\epsilon_{k}
is uncorrelated with every function of
X_{S_{k}}
conditionally on
X_{S_{\cap}}
, and

{\rm Var}_{P}(\tau^{\mathtt{AIPW}}_{k}(Y,A,X)\mid X_{S_{\cap}})={\rm Var}_{P}(\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}})+\mathbb{E}_{P}[\epsilon_{k}^{2}\mid X_{S_{\cap}}].

Since
A(1-A)=0
,

\mathbb{E}_{P}[\epsilon_{k}^{2}\mid X_{S_{k}}]=\frac{{\rm Var}_{P}(Y\mid A=1,X_{S_{k}})}{e(X_{S_{k}})}+\frac{{\rm Var}_{P}(Y\mid A=0,X_{S_{k}})}{1-e(X_{S_{k}})}\leq\frac{2C}{\xi}.

Combining the above with (a) gives the first bound in (b). The second bound simply follows from the inequality
{\rm Var}(U-V\mid X_{S_{\cap}})\leq 2{\rm Var}(U\mid X_{S_{\cap}})+2{\rm Var}(V\mid X_{S_{\cap}})
.
\square

(c) Let
m_{k}(X_{S_{\cap}})=\mathbb{E}_{P}[\tau(X_{S_{k}})\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]
. Using the tower property, conditional Jensen’s inequality and part (a),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}|\tau(X_{S_{k}})|]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}|m_{k}|]+\mathbb{E}_{P}\big[w^{*}\,\mathbb{E}_{P}[|\tau(X_{S_{k}})-m_{k}|\mid X_{S_{\cap}}]\big]

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*})^{2}])^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}_{P}[m_{k}^{2}])^{1/2}+(4C/\xi)^{1/2},

since
\mathbb{E}_{P}[w^{*}]=1
. Condition (iii) of
Theorem

3.2
implies that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[(w^{*})^{2}]=\psi_{P}(2\lambda^{*})/\psi_{P}(\lambda^{*})^{2}<\infty
, and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[m_{k}^{2}]<\infty
follows from the fact that
m_{1}
and
g
have finite moment generating functions in an open neighborhood of the origin and
m_{k}=m_{1}-g_{k}
. This completes the proof.

\square
